The single-sourced `MERGE_GATES_BLOCK_ALLOWLIST_RE` keeps the block-list in sync, but the label-DOWNGRADE set is hand-maintained in two places.

Fixed `evaluate_rollup` (Plan-lock gate downgrade in both the row-blocker and absent-required filters, + selftest CASE4c), and `test-oob-label-impl.sh` now mechanically extracts both scripts' CI-downgrade label sets (label→jq-var bindings resolved against each downgrade expression) and FAILs on inequality — `--selftest` divergence fixture + bats coverage in `tests/bats/oob_label_impl.bats` (divergent FAIL, equal PASS, bound-but-unused-is-not-wiring, real-tree parity).
- 2026-06-20 · orchestrator (backlog reconciliation) · [process] · P3 — ad-hoc "open backlog" recounts with `grep -rh … | grep -v applied.md` silently read applied.md as live (grep -h strips the filename, so the applied.md exclusion is a no-op) — twice misled a session into "fixing" already-fixed work
  Details: During a session I twice computed "what P1s are open" with `grep -rhE '· P1 —' categories/*.md categories/*/*.md | grep -v applied.md`. Because `-h` strips the filename from each line, the `grep -v applied.md` filter matches nothing and is a no-op — so every archived entry in `applied.md` was counted as live. This produced a phantom list of ~20 "open" P1s (sanitizer-required-context, coverage-required-context, two security P1s, …) that were ALL already fixed + archived. Acting on it, I scoped a "fix the 2 security P1s" task that turned out fully done (verified #1210 + #1211 live). A 4-agent verification fan-out then confirmed ZERO genuinely-open entries were stale-resolved — the backlog `Status` fields are accurate; only my recount lied. The repo's own `test-backlog-counts.sh --list` is fine (per-file, no `-h` bug) — this is purely an ad-hoc-survey methodology trap.
  Concrete next action: when recounting backlog status ad-hoc, never rely on `grep -h … | grep -v applied.md`. Use a per-file loop (`for f in …; case applied.md skip`) or filename-aware `grep -rHn` then exclude by `/applied.md:` path, and pair the header match with the entry's actual `Status:` line (a header line alone says nothing about open/applied). Prefer `test-backlog-counts.sh --list` for category totals. This is a doc/habit note — no gate needed (the official tooling is already correct).
  Resolution: applied — methodology note added to docs/agent-rules/process-rules.md § Cadence and verification ('Ad-hoc backlog recounts must be filename-aware'): grep -rHn + /applied.md: path exclusion + pair each header hit with its Status: line, or use test-backlog-counts.sh --list.
  Status: applied
  Last-reviewed: 2026-07-08

- 2026-06-20 · orchestrator · [process] · P2 — MCP-created PRs bypass the `## Intent` template → required gate fails, not self-healing
  Details: ship-loops.md § Intent capture says fill the PR body's `## Intent` at "`gh pr create` time" from `.github/pull_request_template.md`. But the remote/web harness opens PRs via the GitHub MCP `create_pull_request` tool, which sets `body` verbatim and does NOT apply the repo PR template (template auto-fill is a web-UI / interactive `gh pr create` behaviour only). So an MCP-opened PR starts with no `## Intent` and the REQUIRED "Intent section" check (`doc-validation.yml`) fails on `opened`. Worse: that workflow's `pull_request:` trigger declares no `types:`, so it defaults to `[opened, synchronize, reopened]` — editing the PR body does NOT re-run it; the red required check only clears on a NEW push (`synchronize`). Hit this session on PR #1460 (the check cleared only because a later commit re-triggered the workflow against the corrected body).
  Concrete next action: in ship-loops.md § Intent capture (+ the orchestrator PR-create packet), state that a PR created via the GitHub MCP `create_pull_request` MUST include the `## Intent` section in the `body` argument explicitly (the template is not auto-applied), and that a missing-Intent PR needs a follow-up commit to clear the gate (a body edit alone won't, since it doesn't fire on `edited`). Optional belt-and-braces: add `edited` to `doc-validation.yml`'s `pull_request: types:` so body-only fixes self-heal.
  Status: applied (stale `Status: open` reconciled during the 2026-08-12 archival sweep — entry already lived in applied.md)
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-20

    (the data PR that surfaced it);

- 2026-06-20 · orchestrator (roadmap PR-13c) · [debt] · P3 — the `imgui-define-macro` cleanup is UNSOUND as originally specified — replacing `#define ImGui SmatchetLocalizedImGui` with `using namespace SmatchetLocalizedImGui;` would silently bypass localization across ~40 TUs
  Details: PR-13a (#1515) halted the `imgui-define-macro` deliverable after finding the premise wrong. Every call site is the QUALIFIED name `ImGui::Foo`; the `#define` textually rewrites it to `SmatchetLocalizedImGui::Foo`, but a `using namespace` directive does NOT redirect qualified-name lookup — so `ImGui::Foo` would resolve to the real `::ImGui::Foo`, compiling clean while skipping the localization overrides (`LabelFromSource`/`TranslateSource`/`WindowTitleFromSource`). 40 TUs carry the macro; `SmatchetUI_Internal.h` documents the qualified-rewrite as deliberate. A clean-compiling silent i18n regression — worse than a build break.
  Concrete next action: RESPEC or DROP. Option A (drop): keep the macro — it is a deliberate, documented localization mechanism; close this as won't-fix. Option B (respec): convert all `ImGui::Foo` call sites to UNQUALIFIED `Foo(...)` form across the 40 TUs (~168 sites in `SmatchetAiAssistantUi.cpp` alone) + add the using-directive + verify localization coverage didn't drop (a grep/test that every localized override is still reached). Option B is a large mechanical sweep with a real regression-risk surface; only worth it if the macro is causing a concrete problem. Recommend A unless a problem is named. Cross-ref: PR-13a #1515; `SmatchetLocalizedImGui.h`; `SmatchetUI_Internal.h:6-7`.
  Status: wont-fix (2026-06-21 decision — keep the macro) — the `#define ImGui SmatchetLocalizedImGui` is a deliberate, documented localization mechanism; replacing it with `using namespace` is unsound (cannot redirect qualified `ImGui::` names → would silently bypass localization across 40 TUs), and a full call-site conversion is a large risky sweep with no functional gain. Decision: keep the macro.
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-21
- 2026-06-19 · orchestrator · [process] · P2 — block-allowlisted `Intent section` gate is bypassed by every non-poller merge path (bare `gh pr merge --auto`, direct REST); promote the 2026-06-11 "use the poller" advice into an enforced non-admin merge wrapper
  Details: Three PRs (#1414, #1415, #1406) merged to develop in one 07:55–07:58Z batch with the
    `Intent section` doc-validation check non-green and no `intent-out-of-band` override label —
    see postmortems.md 2026-06-19 PR #1406, #1414, #1415. `Intent section` is on the merge-gates.sh
    `MERGE_GATES_BLOCK_ALLOWLIST_RE` *meant-to-block* allow-list (added 2026-06-18, ADR-0022) but is
    deliberately NOT a develop branch-protection required context, so it is enforced ONLY by the
    merge-gates poller / watcher. Bare native `gh pr merge --auto` (#1414, #1406) waits only on the
    branch-protection required contexts (Test-delta, Windows+MSVC ×2, Shell lint, Doc anchors,
    Perf PR-fast, Coverage, Sanitizer ×2 — Intent is not among them) and merges past a red Intent the
    instant those green; a direct REST `PUT …/merge` (#1415) polls nothing at all. This is the exact
    sharp edge already documented for CodeRabbit in the 2026-06-11 process.md entry ("raw `--auto` only
    waits on the required status checks") — its remedy was advisory ("DEFAULT to the merge-gates poller
    path"), and the 3× recurrence in one batch shows advisory guidance does not hold under autonomous
    ship-loops. (#1406 is a stale-red member — its `## Intent` is filled now; the failed run predated the
    fill — but it merged via the same `--auto` bypass and the detector nags it under the same trigger.)
  Concrete next action: add `agents/scripts/core/safe-merge.sh` — a non-admin sibling of
    `safe-admin-merge.sh` that (1) runs `merge-gates.sh` for the PR (which already blocks on the full
    block-allowlist incl. `Intent section` / CodeRabbit), (2) arms `gh pr merge --squash --auto` ONLY on a
    PASS, and (3) refuses on any red block-allowlist gate lacking its `*-out-of-band` override label. Make
    it the single sanctioned agent merge entry-point; update `docs/agent-rules/ship-loops.md` +
    `docs/agent-rules/merge-gates.md` to forbid bare `gh pr merge --auto` and direct REST `PUT …/merge` in
    the ship-loop. Back it with a bats test (sibling of `test-safe-admin-merge-bats.sh`) asserting refusal
    when a block-allowlist gate is red without its override label, and PASS-then-arm when green/overridden.
    Est ~0.5–1d. ADR-0022 keeps Intent off branch-protection required-contexts on purpose (merge-queue
    deadlock reversibility), so enforcement must stay on the merge-actor side — do NOT "fix" this by
    promoting Intent to a required context.
  Triggered-follow-up: supersedes the advisory remedy in the 2026-06-11 process.md entry "authorized
    auto-merge armed via raw `gh pr merge --auto`" — when this wrapper lands, update that entry's status to
    note the advisory was promoted to an enforced gate.
  Cross-ref: postmortems.md 2026-06-19 PR #1406, #1414, #1415; merge commits 96e79412 (#1414),
    7bb77daa (#1406), f6bb3972 (#1415); merge-gates.sh `MERGE_GATES_BLOCK_ALLOWLIST_RE`;
    docs/agent-rules/merge-gates.md:84; docs/adr/0022-intent-gate-promotion.md; safe-admin-merge.sh.
  Status: applied (2026-06-20 roadmap campaign — shipped #1503)
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-20

- 2026-06-19 · orchestrator (gate-escape postmortem, PR #1428) · [tooling] · P1 — the `smatchet-merge-watcher` daemon runs `merge-gates.sh` from its own long-lived host checkout, so a daemon started days ago silently enforces STALE gate logic (block-allowlist / CI rules) that has since changed on `develop`. It merged #1428 past a red block-allowlisted `Intent section` check because its checkout predated the #1391/ADR-0022 allow-list addition; the poller *was* consulted but ran two-day-old rules, and the `merge-snapshots.jsonl` audit row (written by the stale poller itself) self-reported `redChecks:[]`, so the audit trail cannot detect its own staleness.
  Details: the watcher (`agents/scripts/core/merge-watcher.py`, Scheduled Task `SmatchetMergeWatcher`) ran `merge-gates.sh` from the integration tree `C:/Dev/Smatchet`, parked on a branch predating #1391. `Intent section` was added to `MERGE_GATES_BLOCK_ALLOWLIST_RE` on 2026-06-18 (#1391, ADR-0022); the daemon's blob predated that, so its regex did not match `Intent section` → a non-required RED treated as advisory → `GATES_PASSED`. Distinct from the #1406/#1414/#1415 escape (poller never consulted; remedy = poll-gated merge wrapper) — here the poller WAS consulted, so a wrapper would invoke the same stale poller and not catch it.
  Preventing gate (applied): a gate-logic self-freshness guard in `agents/scripts/core/merge-gates.sh` — before emitting `GATES_PASSED` it compares the git blob of its own running file against `origin/develop`'s blob for the same path and, on divergence (or when unverifiable), refuses `GATES_PASSED` fail-closed. Gated by `MERGE_GATES_FRESHNESS` ∈ `{off (default) | warn | block}`; `merge-watcher.py` sets `block`; default `off` leaves existing callers + local-dev unaffected. Backed by 5 bats cases (off-inert, block+stale→refuse, block+fresh→pass, warn→warn-only, block+unverifiable→fail-closed).
  Residual (operational): a self-guard cannot retro-protect a daemon already running the pre-guard file, so the stale daemon was stopped + its Scheduled Task disabled (fail-safe), to be restarted only from an up-to-date `develop` checkout, plus a periodic daemon self-resync. That operational restart-from-fresh-checkout step is the open residual.
  Cross-ref: postmortem [`postmortems.md`](../postmortems.md) (2026-06-19 · PR #1428); reconciled in [`applied.md`](applied.md); ADR-0022; #1391.
  Status: applied (freshness self-guard + bats shipped in #1428; the restart-from-fresh-checkout / periodic-resync residual is operational and tracked here + in applied.md).
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-24

- 2026-06-19 · orchestrator · [tooling] · P3 — `gate-script-into-pretooluse-hook-needs-threshold-and-own-output-assertions`: promoting a `--strict` gate-script (built for explicit pre-launch invocation, where it flags EVERY instance) into an always-on PreToolUse hook needs a scope threshold or it blocks trivial calls — plus two adjacent gotchas that surfaced wiring it.
  Details: from #1429 (the fleet-preflight `Workflow` PreToolUse hook). Three concrete lessons. (1) THRESHOLD — `fleet-preflight.sh --strict` exits non-zero on ANY warn, and its check 1 flags every unpinned `agent()`, so blocking the Workflow tool on a raw non-zero exit would refuse even a 1-agent helper. The documented MUST is ">2 agents", so the hook must parse the `agent()` count and only block ABOVE that threshold; the gate's own exit code is necessary but not sufficient as a per-call block signal. (2) TEST ASSERTIONS — a hook that ECHOES the wrapped tool's output must assert on its OWN distinctive prefix, not a generic phrase: a bats `[[ "$output" != *"blocking launch"* ]]` false-failed because `fleet-preflight`'s own output also contains "blocking launch"; fixed by asserting the hook-specific `[fleet-preflight-hook] blocking launch`. (3) PORTABLE-PURITY — documenting the hook's `<PROJECT>_*`-prefixed env knobs in the PORTABLE rule-doc (`docs/agent-rules/workflow-fleets.md`) tripped `test-portable-purity` (project-name literal leak); the env-name literals belong in the non-portable hook header, and the portable doc references them generically.
  Concrete next action: when next wiring a gate-script into a per-call PreToolUse/PostToolUse hook, follow this checklist (encode it wherever hook-authoring is documented — the harness `setup.md` or a new `docs/agent-rules` hook note): (a) gate the BLOCK on the documented scope threshold, not the raw gate exit code; (b) make the hook fail-OPEN (missing dep / unparseable payload / wrong tool → exit 0 allow) — a guard that blocks every call when jq is absent is worse than the gap it closes; (c) assert tests on the hook's own output prefix, never a phrase the wrapped tool also emits; (d) keep project-prefixed env-name literals out of portable rule-docs (reference generically, literal lives in the non-portable source). Est: nil beyond the doc note — these are discipline, proven by the #1429 bats.
  Cross-ref: #1429 (`pretool-workflow-fleet-preflight.sh`, `tests/bats/pretool_workflow_fleet_preflight.bats`); `docs/harness/claude-code/hooks/pretool-edit-p4-lock-check.sh` (the block/downgrade pattern mirrored); `agents/scripts/core/fleet-preflight.sh`; `agents/scripts/core/test-portable-purity.sh`; the parent backlog entry `fleet-preflight-not-enforced-at-launch` (tooling.md).
  Resolution: applied — the 4-point hook-authoring checklist is now a durable section in docs/harness/SETUP.md § Hook-authoring checklist (threshold-gated block; fail-OPEN on plumbing; own-output-prefix test assertions; no project-prefixed env literals in portable docs), and lint-portable-purity.sh's header now cites that section instead of this backlog entry.
  Status: applied
  Last-reviewed: 2026-07-08

- 2026-06-19 · orchestrator (fan-in json-door sweep) · [debt] — json_fwd swap of the two top-fan-in by-value json carriers (Ui/SmatchetUiSession.h + Commands/Command.h) to drop full <nlohmann/json.hpp> off their transitive include edges
  Resolution: BOTH halves shipped. (1) Ui/SmatchetUiSession.h via #1425 — kept its two std::vector<nlohmann::json> members (incomplete element type is fine for a vector member; out-of-line ~UiDrawSession localises the only tree-wide ~vector<json>), swapped to json_fwd, added direct json.hpp to the 7 use/construct TUs. (2) Commands/Command.h — boxed the three SCALAR by-value members (ParamSpec::Default, CommandError::Details, CommandResult::Data) behind std::shared_ptr<nlohmann::json> so the header needs only json_fwd; shared_ptr keeps Command/ParamSpec/CommandResult copyable with compiler-generated special members (the registry's All() returns vector<Command> by value; the palette copies it) — no hand-written rule-of-five. ~29 access sites updated with null-pointer guards; Success()/Failure() factories absorb the boxing so handlers calling Success(data) are unchanged; added direct json.hpp to the registration/consumer TUs. Verified on both halves via whole-tree first-party include-closure walk (json.hpp gone from each header's closure, zero uncovered fallout) + shared_ptr<json>/vector<json> + json_fwd isolation-compile on g++ 13 and clang++ 18 at -std=c++14 (pinned nlohmann v3.11.3). Standalone-only compile-cost win (the DX12 PCH still precompiles json.hpp). AppController.h's json door was already closed by appcontroller-fan-in, so the json/header-choke from build-quality-velocity-hardening § Root-multiplier-A is now resolved for all three named by-value carriers.

- 2026-06-19 · orchestrator · [tooling] · P3 — postmortem-owed.sh reads the cwd working-tree ledger instead of origin/develop, so a session whose integration tree is parked on a feature branch gets phantom "owed" nags (and, inversely, a locally-staged-but-unmerged entry can false-suppress a genuinely-owed postmortem)
  Details: The 2026-06-19 session-start postmortem-owed hook reported `PR #1409 — override:
    tests-out-of-band` owed, but that postmortem was already merged to develop — #1414, squash
    96e79412, entry `## 2026-06-19 · PR #1390, #1409` in postmortems.md. The detector ran from the
    integration tree (C:/Dev/Smatchet) which was checked out on feat/tsan-subset-sync-layer, a branch
    predating #1414. has_entry / has_sha_entry grep `LEDGER` (postmortem-owed.sh:64 default
    `docs/self-improvement/postmortems.md`, used at :124 and :143) as a cwd-relative WORKING-TREE path,
    so they read the stale checked-out file (0 matches) and nagged. Re-running with POSTMORTEM_LEDGER
    pointed at `git show origin/develop:…postmortems.md` reported `no gate escapes owed (last 20 merges
    clean)`. The false-positive direction is just startup noise; the dangerous inverse is a
    false-NEGATIVE — if the working tree holds a postmortem entry that is NOT yet merged to develop,
    has_entry returns true and silently suppresses a genuinely-owed postmortem on develop. The detector
    already scans the merges themselves against origin/develop (`git log origin/develop …` at :448/:492),
    so only the ledger read diverges from the ref everything else trusts.
  Concrete next action: pin the ledger read to the same ref the merges are scanned against. In
    has_entry / has_sha_entry, resolve the ledger via `git show "origin/develop:$LEDGER"` (cache once to
    a temp file, grep that) instead of reading the cwd working-tree path; keep POSTMORTEM_LEDGER as an
    explicit override so the bats rig can still point at a fixture. Add bats cases (sibling of the
    existing postmortem-owed tests): (1) working tree on a branch LACKING the entry while origin/develop
    HAS it → detector reports clean, no phantom owe; (2) entry present ONLY in the working tree, absent
    on origin/develop → still owes (guards the false-negative). Est ~0.5d.
  Cross-ref: postmortem-owed.sh:64 (LEDGER default), :124-133 (has_entry), :143-146 (has_sha_entry),
    :448/:492 (`git log origin/develop` merge scan); postmortems.md 2026-06-19 PR #1390, #1409 (the entry
    falsely reported owed); merge 96e79412 (#1414); session-start postmortem-owed hook.
  Resolution: applied — when POSTMORTEM_LEDGER is unset, LEDGER now resolves via
    `git show "origin/develop:$LEDGER"` cached once to a mktemp file (rm on EXIT),
    so has_entry / has_sha_entry grep the same ref the merge scans trust; the
    working-tree path is only the degraded fallback when origin/develop is
    unresolvable, and POSTMORTEM_LEDGER stays the explicit override for the bats
    rig. Two new bats cases guard both directions (entry on origin/develop only →
    no phantom owe; entry in the working tree only → still owes).
  Status: applied
  Last-reviewed: 2026-07-08

  Details: #1370's `--spawn` exit-code fix compiled clean + ctest 7/7, but a single bare `///` opening a doc-comment tripped `comment-decorative-banner` in `comment_audit --diff origin/develop`, failing the required Windows+MSVC job. Because bucket-C/E/mobile-texture-guard all `needs: windows-msvc`, ALL THREE skipped — so the launch-smoke step (the only thing that could verify the spawn fix) never ran. An agent authoring C++ doc comments can thus red the whole pipeline AND block the verification it needs, on a pure style nit, with the failure buried in a sub-step of a build job.
  Concrete next action: (1) agents that edit first-party C++ MUST run `bash agents/scripts/project/test-lint-rules.sh --diff origin/develop` locally before pushing — add this to the code-editing subagent prompts (build-doctor, mechanic, debug-detective, the decompose/edit playbook). (2) Evaluate whether the comment-noise gate belongs inside the required build at all vs its own advisory lane, so a style nit doesn't skip the bucket jobs. Est ~20 min (prompt note) / ~1 h (CI restructure). Cross-ref: this session #1370; `.github/workflows/build-and-test.yml` "Run non-UI bucket-A tests" step.
  Resolution: applied — the comment-noise lint was broken out of the windows-msvc build job into its own required `comment-noise-gate` lane (`.github/workflows/build-and-test.yml:243`, with a tombstone note at the old bucket-A step), so a style nit no longer reds the build or skips downstream bucket jobs; pre-push `test-lint-rules.sh --diff origin/develop` notes landed in `agents/core/{build-doctor,debug-detective,mechanic,test-rig}.md` and `docs/agent-rules/delegation.md`.

- 2026-06-18 · claude (dx12-arm64 Phase 4 session) · [tooling] · P2 — `plan-index-autosync-PAT-403`: the `Auto-sync plan INDEX` job cannot self-heal — its `PLAN_INDEX_PAT` push 403s, so any genuine shipped-index drift reds every PR's doc gate
  Details: the "Auto-sync plan INDEX (PR branch)" job regenerates `docs/plans/INDEX.md` (`test-plan-index.sh --fix`) and, on drift, commits + pushes the result via `PLAN_INDEX_PAT`. Observed on PR #1389: the push failed `remote: Permission to alexandrosk0/Smatchet.git denied to alexandrosk0 ... 403`, so the auto-fix path dies and the companion `Doc anchors + agent contract` job (which runs `test-plan-index.sh` non-fix) reds the doc gate. In #1389 the index was actually in sync — the apparent drift was a self-inflicted shallow-clone regen (the index sorts by `git log --follow` first-commit dates, which a shallow checkout computes wrong) — so it did not block. But a real shipped-plan add/rename would wedge every open PR until someone regenerates from FULL history and pushes by hand. The PAT is likely expired, missing `Contents: write`, or not granted to this repo.
  Concrete next action: rotate/re-scope `PLAN_INDEX_PAT` (fine-grained PAT, Contents: Read+write, this repo) so the auto-sync push succeeds; OR convert the job to fail-with-remedy only (no push) and add a header note that the author must regenerate locally with full history (`git fetch --unshallow` first — a shallow regen corrupts the date sort). ~15 min once PAT access is decided.

  Details: the Phase 4 PR adds `windows-msvc-arm64`, an advisory compile-only `amd64_arm64` cross job (`msvc-dev-cmd arch: amd64_arm64` then `ninja-iter-msvc-arm64` builds `SmatchetStandalone`). The dormant `ENABLE_WIN_ARM64_RUNNER` gate stays in place so the leg is a one-variable toggle if the runner tier ever changes. One NEW residual emerged (the bootstrapped `ci-windows-arm64` perf-baseline SEED FILES can't be fetched + committed from the sandbox — egress allowlist blocks `*.blob.core.windows.net`); filed separately under `categories/tooling/` as `arm64-perf-baseline-commit-blocked-on-sandbox-egress`.

- 2026-06-18 · orchestrator (PR-1358 ship session) · [tooling] · P2 — `guard-shared-tree-matcher-greps-whole-command-string-false-positives-on-heredoc-and-quoted-git-verbs`: the shared-tree guard decides "this is a mutating git op" by grepping the ENTIRE tool command string for a `git <verb>` token sequence, so any command whose literal text merely CONTAINS the words `git checkout/pull/reset/merge/rebase` — inside a heredoc body, a quoted string, an `echo`, or a comment — is denied as if it were really moving HEAD, even when the command runs no git at all
  Details: the mutating-op gate at `guard-shared-tree.sh:69` is a single `printf '%s' "$CMD" | grep -qE '(^|[;&|[:space:]])git(...flags...)*[[:space:]]+(checkout|switch|pull|reset|merge|rebase)($|[[:space:]])|...git stash pop...'` over the whole command string `$CMD` (read from `.tool_input.command`, :66). The regex anchors `git` to a shell-token boundary (`^` / `;` / `&` / `|` / whitespace) but has no notion of heredoc bodies, quoted literals, or comments — those are all still part of `$CMD`, so a `git merge` appearing in DOCUMENTATION text matches exactly like a `git merge` being executed. Lived this session, twice, while filing these very self-improvement entries: appending an entry whose prose documents `git merge`/`rebase`/`reset` via `cat <<'EOF' >> docs/.../tooling.md` was DENIED with the standard liveness reason (":98"), because the heredoc BODY tripped :69 — no git command was being run, only text written. (This is the THIRD occurrence: the same trap fires again on THIS entry's body.) Distinct from the sibling P3 `guard-shared-tree-deny-message-omits-git-C-exemption` filed alongside it: that one is about the deny MESSAGE's wording (it doesn't advertise the working `git -C` form); THIS one is about the MATCHER's scope (it classifies on command-string content, not on the actual command/argv being executed) — different root cause, different fix. The `-C <linked-worktree>` exemption (:79-86) does not help here either, since these commands carry no `-C` token. Net effect: an agent cannot write docs that mention git verbs into the shared tree via a heredoc; the workaround that unblocked me was to Write the body to a temp file OUTSIDE the repo, then `cat <tmpfile> >> <target>` (that command's text contains only paths, so :69 sees no git-verb token).
  Concrete next action: stop matching on raw command-string content. Cheapest robust fix — before the :69 grep, strip heredoc bodies from `$CMD` (drop everything between a `<<['"]?WORD` opener and its closing `WORD` line) so only the actual command line is classified; this kills the heredoc false-positive without a full shell parser. Add a `tests/bats/guard_shared_tree.bats` case: a `cat <<'EOF' >> file` whose body contains the literal "git merge" must NOT be denied (and a real `cd <shared-tree> && git merge` still IS). Interim doc remedy (no code, ~5 min): record the temp-file + `cat >>` workaround in `docs/agent-rules/process-rules.md` near the shared-worktree discipline section so the next agent isn't blocked writing git-mentioning docs. Est ~45-60 min for the heredoc-strip + bats; ~5 min for the doc remedy. It (a) never names the exempt `git -C <worktree-path>` form, and (b) presents the override as if an inline `SMATCHET_ALLOW_SHARED_SWITCH=1 git …` prefix would work — it does not, because the hook reads its own process env, not the bash command's inline assignment. Cross-ref: applied.md 2026-06-11 `guard-shared-tree-blocks-git-C-worktree` (the fix that ADDED the `-C` exemption this message still fails to advertise); the guard-head-drift Edit/Write-arm worktree-awareness gap (tooling.md — sibling guard, same `-C`-token shape); #1373 process `shared-tree-worktree-edit-clobber-and-ps1-bypass` (sibling shared-tree / worktree friction); process-rules.md:50 (`git -C <path>` not `cd` for shared-worktree ops) + :69-72 (§ Concurrent interactive sessions — the guard-head-drift `git -C` linked-worktree exemption this mirrors).

  Details: hit ~4× this session (every `git commit` / `git checkout -b` from a `.claude/worktrees/<id>` worktree first denied with "N concurrent sessions share this integration tree … would change HEAD/working-tree under them", then succeeded once the `-C` arg was a literal absolute path) AND independently by build-doctor on #1375 (same root cause, its self-improvement note). The guard (`guard-shared-tree.sh` ~line 79) decides "this op targets a linked worktree → allow" by regex-extracting the `-C` path from the literal command text; a shell `$VAR` isn't expanded in the hook's view, and a preceding `cd <shared-tree>` makes a `-C <worktree>` op read as shared-tree-relative — both fall through to the fail-closed deny. Workaround that always works: pass the LITERAL absolute worktree path to `git -C` (no variable) and don't `cd` into the shared tree first.
  Concrete next action: make the exemption resolve the op's EFFECTIVE worktree instead of regex-matching the raw `-C` token — e.g. shell-expand then `git -C <path> rev-parse --show-toplevel --git-common-dir` and allow when the toplevel is a registered linked worktree of the common dir. Cross-ref: build-doctor #1375 self-improvement note; this session's repeated commit/checkout denials; `agents/scripts/.../guard-shared-tree.sh`.

- 2026-06-18 · orchestrator (PR-1358 ship session) · [infra] · P2 — `merge-watcher-no-update-branch-for-standalone-behind-pr`: a green + CR-cleared REGISTERED PR that drifts BEHIND base on a busy develop is never auto-advanced — the daemon classifies BEHIND and escalates a human notify, but its ONLY `update-branch` path is the post-merge cascade for stacked CHILDREN, so a standalone BEHIND PR starves until a manual `gh pr update-branch`
  Details: `_classify_pr_wedge` returns `"BEHIND"` for a behind-base PR once CI is confirmed not-pending (`agents/scripts/core/merge-watcher.py:1827`; reason text :1739 "branch is behind base ... needs update-branch / rebase"). Its caller — the stuck-streak escalator (:1905-1945) — does NOT advance the PR: it bumps a `stuck_streak` and at threshold flips `last_state` to `STUCK_NEEDS_ATTENTION` + fires one `maybe_notify` (a HUMAN toast), then waits. `maybe_auto_act` (:2111-2160) only spawns a remediation session on a CR-finding (`TRIAGE_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` / CR-block) or sanitizer trigger — never on BEHIND. The daemon's sole `update-branch` dispatch is `cascade_update_child` (:833), called from exactly ONE site — the post-merge cascade (:2447-2456) that walks `find_stacked_children` of a freshly-merged PARENT. So a standalone registered PR (not a stacked child of anything currently merging) that is green + CR-cleared but BEHIND base is NEVER auto-advanced by the watcher; branch protection's "require branches up to date" turns every develop advance into a fresh BEHIND, and the PR sits BLOCKED indefinitely. Lived this session: #1358 (`feat/admin-merge-cr-wait`) — green, CR-cleared, registered (`active.json`: 1357, 1358) — read `mergeStateStatus=BLOCKED` for ~24 h while the watcher squash-merged sibling PRs (#1368-1372); a manual server-side update-branch (`gh api PUT .../update-branch`) cleared it, then it re-drifted to BLOCKED a SECOND time within ~24 h (develop advanced past head `40675e96` again), needing yet another manual update-branch. Distinct from #1373 process `green-pr-blocked-no-merge-signal` (that block was an unresolved bot review-thread under `require_conversation_resolution`; this is the up-to-date-branch requirement + the watcher's structural non-advance) and from the 2026-06-02 `triage_attempts`-unbounded entry below (that PR was DIRTY / CR-findings-latched, not clean-BEHIND).
  Concrete next action: give the daemon a bounded auto-update-branch for a registered PR whose ONLY blocker is BEHIND — when `_classify_pr_wedge` returns `"BEHIND"` AND the rollup is green + CR-cleared, dispatch the server-side `gh api PUT .../update-branch` (reuse the `cascade_update_child` helper at :833) instead of only escalating; gate it behind an opt-in knob mirroring the existing auto-act env (`MERGE_WATCH_AUTO_UPDATE_BEHIND=true`) and cap it per-PR-lifetime like `MERGE_WATCH_AUTO_ACT_BUDGET` (default 2) keyed on an `auto_update_for_head_sha` dedup field, so a hot develop can't trigger whack-a-mole update-branch churn. Add a `tests/bats/merge_watcher.bats` case: a registered PR classified BEHIND + green → one update-branch dispatched; a second within the same head_sha (or past budget) suppressed. Cheaper interim (no code): make the `STUCK_NEEDS_ATTENTION` notify text name `safe-admin-merge.sh <pr>` as the operator remedy for a BEHIND-all-green wedge (it already handles STALE-BLOCKED / BEHIND-all-green — ship-loops.md:161-163). Est ~1.5-2 h for the daemon path + bats; ~15 min for the notify-text remedy. Cross-ref: infra.md 2026-06-15 `daemon-loop-per-iteration-backstop-audit` (its Layer-4 hardens this same `cascade_update_child` path); infra.md 2026-06-02 `triage_attempts`-unbounded (sibling: a registered PR the watcher can't advance → manual merge); tooling.md 2026-05-31 (`SmatchetMergeWatcher` can notify the human but has no channel to wake the agent — same "watcher escalates, doesn't remediate" shape); #1373 process `green-pr-blocked-no-merge-signal` (sibling green+BLOCKED, different blocker); ship-loops.md:161-165 (the admin-merge carve-out for STALE-BLOCKED / BEHIND-all-green). Incident: PR #1358.
  Resolution (2026-06-18, #1393): applied — fix merged to develop.
  Status: applied
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-18

- 2026-06-18 · orchestrator (sec-consent-modal #1400 ship session) · [tooling] · P2 — `comment-blank-run-flags-single-doc-paragraph-separator`: the `comment-blank-run` lint rejects a SINGLE bare `//` line used as a paragraph separator inside one contiguous doc-comment block — there is no allowance for "blank comment line between two prose paragraphs of the same block," so the idiomatic way to break a long header doc into readable paragraphs is a hard CI failure that a green local build does not surface.
  Details: hit on PR #1400 (first-send outbound-context consent modal). The new pure header `Source/Core/include/AiOutboundConsent.h` used two bare `//` lines as paragraph breaks inside an otherwise-contiguous doc-comment block (between the "what it does" prose and the "why it's header-inline" prose). `comment_audit.py` flagged both as new comment-noise vs `origin/develop` and the `test-lint-rules.sh` "Run non-UI bucket-A tests" step (the `Windows + MSVC` required context) failed — a full CI round-trip for two readability separators, while BOTH builds were green locally (the lint does not run in the targeted build-verify path — see the sibling `build-verify-shortcut-bypasses-comment-noise-and-lint-gate`, this file/date). Root cause: `comment_audit.py:58` `DECORATIVE_RE = re.compile(r"^\s*(//|/\*|\*)[\s/*=#~_>-]*$")` matches a bare `//` (the alternative is empty after the comment opener) → bucket `cut-blank` (one of `CUT_BUCKETS`) → rule `comment-blank-run` (name mapping ~line 281). The classifier has no notion of "this bare `//` sits between two non-blank comment lines of the same block" — it treats every bare `//` as strippable noise regardless of context. `tests/` is excluded, so the same separators in the test file were NOT flagged; only `Source/`-tree headers trip it. The fix this session was to delete both separators and let the prose run together (commit 782e23ce), which is strictly worse for readability than the intended paragraph break.
  Concrete next action: relax the `cut-blank` classification so a SINGLE bare `//` (or `*`) line that sits BETWEEN two non-blank comment lines of the SAME contiguous block is treated as an allowed intra-block paragraph separator, not noise. Keep flagging (a) runs of 2+ consecutive bare comment lines, (b) decorative banners (the `[=#~_>-]` fill variants), and (c) a bare `//` that brackets commented-out code. Implementation: in the line-walk that assigns `cut-blank`, look at the prev/next non-empty line — if both are in-block comment lines and this is the only blank-comment line in the run, skip the cut. Add a `--selftest` fixture pinning: single separator between two prose lines = PASS; 2+ run = still cut; leading/trailing block separator = still cut. Est ~1–1.5 h (classifier tweak in `comment_audit.py` + selftest cases + re-run `test-lint-rules.sh --selftest`). Cross-ref: PR #1400 (the incident + commit 782e23ce that worked around it by deleting the separators); `build-verify-shortcut-bypasses-comment-noise-and-lint-gate` (the shift-left half — why a green local build hid this); `agents/scripts/core/comment_audit.py:58` (`DECORATIVE_RE`) + ~line 281 (bucket→rule map).
  Status: applied (2026-06-20 roadmap campaign — shipped #1509)
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-20

- 2026-06-18 · orchestrator (sec-consent-modal #1400 ship session) · [tooling] · P2 — `build-verify-shortcut-bypasses-comment-noise-and-lint-gate`: the standard build-verify loop (a targeted `cmake --build` / `build_and_run.ps1 -BuildOnly` / the ship-loop build step) compiles + runs tests but does NOT run `comment_audit.py` or `test-lint-rules.sh`, so a fully-green local build can hide a lint-only CI failure (comment-noise, strict-zone, function-size, include-cycle) that only surfaces after push — a wasted CI round-trip on every lint-shaped miss.
  Details: hit on PR #1400 — both builds green locally (`ninja-test-msvc` 8/8, `ninja-iter-msvc` links clean) yet the FIRST CI run failed on the `comment-blank-run` lint (two bare `//` separators; see the sibling `comment-blank-run-flags-single-doc-paragraph-separator`, this file/date). The mechanism to catch it locally already exists but is gated behind the wrong entry point: `scripts/dev/pre-ship.sh` runs `python3 agents/scripts/core/comment_audit.py --fix "$base_ref"` (line ~227) AND `test-lint-rules.sh --diff <base>` (line ~46), and its own header note (line 5) states that a targeted `cmake --build` / `ctest` does NOT run the comment-noise / lint gate. So an agent that verifies via the fast build-only path (the common case mid-iteration) skips pre-ship entirely and only learns of a lint miss from CI. The comment-noise rule is the visible instance, but the same gap covers every `test-lint-rules.sh` check (strict-zone, function-size, comment-ratio WARN, include-cycle, fan-in ratchet) — all CI-only relative to a build-only verify.
  Concrete next action: shift-left — make the standard build-verify loop run a fast lint pre-flight so a green build cannot hide a lint CI failure. Preferred: have `build_and_run.ps1 -BuildOnly` (and the ship-loop build step) invoke `comment_audit.py --diff <base>` + `test-lint-rules.sh --diff <base>` (diff-only, no `--fix`, so it is read-only + fast — seconds against the changed files) after a successful build and fail the verify with the same finding CI would report; optionally offer `--fix` interactively. Cheaper interim: a `scripts/dev/verify.ps1` wrapper (or a documented one-liner in the ship-loop prompt) that chains build → diff-lint, so agents stop reaching for the bare `cmake --build`. Note: relaxing the comment-blank-run rule (the sibling entry) shrinks ONE class of these misses but does not close the gap — function-size / strict-zone / include-cycle stay CI-only until the lint runs in the local loop, so this entry is independently worth doing. Est ~45 min–1 h (wire the diff-lint into `build_and_run.ps1 -BuildOnly` + a smoke that a comment-noise diff fails the local verify). Cross-ref: PR #1400 (green-build-hid-lint incident); `comment-blank-run-flags-single-doc-paragraph-separator` (the rule-relax half); `scripts/dev/pre-ship.sh` lines ~5/46/227 (the gate that already exists but the build-only path skips).
  Status: applied (2026-06-20 roadmap campaign — shipped #1509)
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-20

  Details: `test-plan-index.sh` sets `ARCHIVE_DIR=docs/plans/shipped` and its Python does `os.listdir(archive_dir)` — relative to the invocation `$PWD`, NOT the git root or the script's own location. - 2026-06-18 · orchestrator (keystore-H2 #1357 session) · [tooling] · P3 — `gh-graphql-reviewthreads-invocation-gotchas`: four concrete Git-Bash/Windows gotchas turn a PR `reviewThreads` query + reply into several failed attempts — implementation notes for the proposed `pr-blocked-why.sh` helper
  Details: Fetching PR #1357's unresolved review threads to verify them against HEAD (see process.md, same date) and posting per-thread replies cost several failed attempts on the orchestrator's Git-Bash/Windows shell before working. Four reusable gotchas, none currently captured: (1) the GitHub repo slug is `alexandrosk0/Smatchet`, NOT the git author display name "Alexandros Konstantonis" — a guessed owner/name fails GraphQL with `Expected NAME, actual: (none)`; derive it once via `gh repo view --json owner,name`. (2) Inlining the multi-line GraphQL on the shell mangles it — write the query to a file and pass `gh api graphql -F query=@file.graphql -F owner=<o> -F name=<r> -F num=<n>`. (3) `gh api` REST endpoints must be passed WITHOUT a leading slash under Git-Bash/MSYS — `repos/<o>/<r>/pulls/<n>/comments/<cid>/replies`, not `/repos/...`; a leading slash is path-rewritten to `C:/Program Files/Git/repos/...` -> "invalid API endpoint". (4) Parsing the JSON in Python defaults to cp1252 on this host and dies on non-ASCII bytes in CR comment bodies (`UnicodeDecodeError ... 0x8f`) — open with `encoding='utf-8'` and ASCII-fold before printing to the cp1252 stdout.
  Concrete next action: encode all four in the `pr-blocked-why.sh` / `reviewThreads` helper proposed by `green-pr-blocked-no-merge-signal` (this file, same date) — slug from `gh repo view`, query via `-F query=@file`, REST paths slash-stripped, JSON read as utf-8 — so the helper runs portably in the orchestrator's Windows/Git-Bash shell.   Status: applied (2026-06-21 P3 sweep — shipped #1525)

- 2026-06-18 · claude (dx12-arm64 ship session) · [tooling] · P2 — `test-plan-index-shallow-clone-corrupts-date-sort`: in a shallow checkout the plan-index generator emits a wrong-but-self-consistent INDEX, so `--fix` writes drift INTO the tree and local `test-docs.sh` passes while CI reds
  Details: `agents/scripts/core/test-plan-index.sh` orders rows + fills the "Approx. date" column from `git log --follow --format=%ad` first-commit dates (:51, sorted at :99). The web/remote execution environment clones SHALLOW (`git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` = true; ~50 commits here), so `--follow` returns the shallow-boundary date instead of the true first-add for any plan whose history predates the boundary → a different row order than CI computes with full history. Lived this session on PR #1389: a clean checkout's committed INDEX looked "drifted" to the shallow generator, I ran `--fix` and committed a 138-line-reordered CORRUPT index; local `test-docs.sh` passed (the shallow regen is internally self-consistent) while CI's `Doc anchors + agent contract` + `Auto-sync plan INDEX` jobs (full history) red'd it. Recovery was `git fetch --unshallow` → re-run → "index up to date (140 plans)" → drop the bad commit + force-push. The high-integrity delta gate already guards this exact class (build-and-test.yml unshallows before `git merge-base`, ~:304), so the index generator is the unguarded sibling — and the agent-side lesson is: never regenerate a `git log`-reading artifact from a shallow clone, and don't "repair" a pre-existing-looking doc-gate red without first confirming it on full history.
  Concrete next action: make `test-plan-index.sh` detect `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository == true` and either auto-`fetch --unshallow` (matching the CI delta-gate pattern) or hard-refuse with "shallow clone: first-commit dates unreliable — unshallow first" instead of silently writing a mis-sorted index; the same guard belongs on any history-reading doc gate. Cheapest: a ~3-line shallow check at the top of the generator + a bats fixture (shallow fixture repo → refuse).
  Status: applied (2026-06-20 roadmap campaign — shipped #1502)
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-20

- 2026-06-18 · orchestrator (pr-intent-capture-hardening session) · [tooling] · P2 — `shallow-clone-corrupts-git-log-date-generators`: `test-plan-index.sh --fix` (and the whole class of `git log`/`git merge-base`-history-dependent generators that PRODUCE committed output) silently writes CORRUPT data in a shallow clone — every history-derived field collapses to the graft-boundary commit — and the remote Claude-Code-on-web container clones shallow by default, so running such a `--fix` there overwrites real content with garbage
  Details: `test-plan-index.sh` derives each shipped-plan row's "Approx. date" from `git log --follow --format=%ad --date=short -- <path>` and takes the LAST line = the first-commit date (`test-plan-index.sh:51`, `_git_first_date_at`). In a shallow clone (`git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` → `true`; the web container clones at a shallow depth — ~52 commits this session) `git log` stops at the graft boundary, so EVERY plan's "first-commit date" resolves to the single graft commit's date. Running `bash test-plan-index.sh --fix` there rewrote all ~140 `docs/plans/INDEX.md` rows to that one graft date and I committed it — turning the deterministic, byte-stable index into uniform garbage. CI then went red on `Doc anchors + agent contract` (date/anchor drift) and the corruption could NOT self-heal: the `autosync-plan-index` job is deliberately NO-PUSH without the opt-in `PLAN_INDEX_PAT` repo secret (`doc-validation.yml:96-205` — a GITHUB_TOKEN bot push wouldn't re-trigger required checks, so by design it prints the drift diff and `exit 1`), so a corrupt COMMITTED index just stays red until the author hand-fixes and re-pushes. Recovered by restoring `docs/plans/INDEX.md` from the pre-corruption blob (`git checkout <good-rev> -- docs/plans/INDEX.md`) then `git fetch --unshallow` so later `git log` reads true history. I also initially MISDIAGNOSED the red as pre-existing upstream drift before tracing it to my own shallow-clone `--fix`. Same ROOT as the applied 2026-06-01 `Delta gates … false-flag … because CI's shallow clone defeats git merge-base` (applied.md:704) but a DISTINCT mechanism + failure mode: that one defeats `merge-base` to false-FLAG findings (read-only advisory noise); this one defeats `git log`-date to CORRUPT generated committed content via a destructive `--fix`. Two different tools bitten by the same shallow-clone root = recurrence, which argues for a shared backstop over a per-tool patch.
  Concrete next action: add a shallow-repo guard to the history-dependent generators — `test-plan-index.sh` (and any sibling that runs `git log --follow` / `git merge-base` to PRODUCE committed output) should `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` and, on `true`, REFUSE `--fix` (exit 2: "shallow clone — run `git fetch --unshallow` first; --fix would collapse every date to the graft boundary") and WARN under `--check`. Cheaper/parallel mitigations: have the remote web harness `git fetch --unshallow` (or `--deepen`) before any agent runs a generator, AND/OR document in the agent-rules that `--fix`-class generators are unsafe in the ephemeral shallow container. A shared `is_shallow_or_refuse()` helper sourced by the generator family closes the class in one place (the merge-base sibling would reuse it). Est ~30-45 min (guard in `test-plan-index.sh` + a bats fixture: a shallow fixture repo → `--fix` refuses; a full clone → unchanged) + ~15 min doc. Cross-ref: `test-plan-index.sh:51` (the `git log --follow` date derivation); applied.md 2026-06-01 `Delta gates … shallow clone defeats git merge-base` (the sibling-root recurrence) + applied.md:361 ("Sibling of the shallow-clone merge-base false-flag"); `doc-validation.yml:96-205` (the autosync no-PAT/no-push design that makes a corrupt COMMITTED index persist red until a human re-push — it amplifies this corruption's cost, and is correct-by-design, NOT a separate bug to file); this session's `docs/plans/INDEX.md` corruption + `git fetch --unshallow` recovery.
  Status: applied (2026-06-20 roadmap campaign — shipped #1502)
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-20

- 2026-06-18 · orchestrator (cluster-A guard-hardening ship session) · [process] · P3 — `pretooluse-git-guard-cwd-not-derivable-from-raw-command`: a PreToolUse git-op guard that exempts based on a parsed `cd <path>` is UNSOUND — shell cwd across `&&`/`||`/`;`/subshells/`cd`-back is not derivable from the raw (pre-execution) command string, so a later op can re-target the protected tree. Key the exemption on an explicit per-op `-C <path>`, verified at command position, never on `cd` parsing.
  Details: Cluster A's first cut at hardening `guard-shared-tree.sh` (PR #1388) added a `cd <worktree>`-then-op exemption; Cursor Bugbot flagged two ordering holes across two review rounds — (1) a `cd`/`-C` attached to a LATER op wrongly exempted an EARLIER bare op that runs in the shared tree (`git reset --hard && cd <wt> && git merge`), and (2) a leading `cd <wt>` exempted a whole compound whose later op re-targets the shared tree (`cd <wt> && git -C <integration> merge`, or `… || git reset`). Also a third: the per-op match initially lacked the command-position prefix, so a verb appearing only as an argument (`… && echo git reset`) forced a false deny. Resolved by DROPPING the cd-exemption entirely (returns to develop's deny-behaviour for `cd && git …`, no regression) and exempting only when EVERY command-position mutating-op invocation carries its own worktree `-C`.
  Concrete next action: add a one-liner to `docs/agent-rules/process-rules.md` § Concurrent interactive sessions near the guard refs — "PreToolUse git-op guards key on explicit `-C <path>` per command-position op; never infer cwd from `cd` in the raw string." Est ~15 min. Cross-ref: PR #1388 (3 fix rounds); Cursor #1388 threads.
  Status: applied (2026-06-21 P3 sweep — shipped #1524)
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-21

- 2026-06-18 · orchestrator · [process] · P2 — `edit-on-merged-pr-branch-reverts-intervening-develop-changes`: committing a follow-up doc edit onto a worktree branch whose PR already MERGED can REGRESS develop (not just orphan the commit), and the documented "fresh branch + cherry-pick" remedy carries the regression forward
  Details: After PR #1392's work, I reused the worktree's checked-out branch (`chore/file-session-learnings-2`, whose PR #1378 had already squash-merged) to mark a backlog entry `applied`. Editing `tooling.md` on that stale base, a diff against `origin/develop` showed develop had ADVANCED the very entry I was touching — the `p99-gate-warmup-frame-exclusion` item was already `partially-applied` on develop, and an unrelated `merge-watcher-register-keyed-by-cwd-clone` entry had been REMOVED — so committing+pushing the stale-base edit would have reverted partially-applied→open and re-added the deleted entry. The pre-push guard (`pre-push: REFUSING push … PR #1378 state: MERGED`) caught it, but only AFTER a full edit+commit was wasted; worse, the documented remedy for an orphaned-after-merge commit (fresh branch off develop + cherry-pick) would have RE-APPLIED the regression, because cherry-pick replays the stale-base hunk, not the current-develop intent. Distinct from the `gh pr merge --auto orphans the follow-up commit` entry (this file, 2026-06-07): that is a commit landing nowhere; this is a stale-base edit silently REVERTING intervening develop changes, where the safe recovery is RE-DERIVING the edit against `git show origin/develop:<file>`, not cherry-picking.
  Concrete next action: (1) before editing any tracked doc in a worktree, confirm the checked-out branch is NOT a merged-PR branch and is NOT behind develop on the target file — `gh pr list --head <branch> --state merged` + `git fetch origin develop && git rev-list --count HEAD..origin/develop -- <file>`; if either is non-empty, branch fresh from origin/develop FIRST. (2) Move the merged-PR push-guard signal EARLIER (a SessionStart / pre-Edit warning when the checked-out branch already has a merged PR) so the wasted edit is prevented, not just the push. (3) When recovering orphaned/stale-base doc work, re-derive against current develop (`git show origin/develop:<file>`), never a blind cherry-pick. Est ~30-45 min for the pre-Edit check; trivial for the habit. Cross-ref: `gh pr merge --auto … orphans the follow-up commit` (this file, 2026-06-07 — the orphan half, whose cherry-pick remedy is UNSAFE for an advanced-base doc); the merged-PR push-guard (the late backstop that caught this).
  Status: applied (2026-06-20 roadmap campaign — shipped #1512)
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-20

- 2026-06-18 · orchestrator · [process] · P3 — `red-required-check-may-be-stale-vs-a-since-merged-develop-fix`: a FAILED required check on an open PR can be a false-red that ran against a pre-fix base — check the failing run's timestamp against subsequent develop merges before diagnosing it as a real regression
  Details: Asked "what needs to be done for #1367" (a Dependabot github-actions bump), I saw its required `Perf PR-fast` lane RED and began reading the perf logs for a real regression. The failure was `cell-edit-burst SmatchetUI::Draw p99Ms 15.217 exceeds ceiling 15.000` — but that run executed at 16:31, BEFORE #1385 (the cell-edit-burst warmup-exclusion fix that removed both that scenario's cold-start pollution AND the 15 ms override) merged to develop at 18:54 the SAME day. So the red was entirely stale: a rebase onto current develop (for Dependabot, an `@dependabot rebase` comment) rebuilds the PR's merge base with #1385 in it and the lane goes green at ~0.3 ms. I nearly diagnosed a long-since-fixed symptom as a live regression. Sibling of `reconfirm-ci-symptom-before-acting-on-filed-diagnosis` (reconfirm from fresh logs) and the `infra.md` clean-BEHIND-PR-not-auto-advanced entry (a GREEN behind PR), but the specific tell here is a RED required check whose run predates a relevant develop merge.
  Concrete next action: when triaging a red required check on an open PR, before treating it as real: (1) read the failing run's start time and compare against `git log --oneline origin/develop --since="<that time>"` for any merge touching the failing area; (2) if a relevant fix merged after the run, update-branch / rebase (Dependabot: `@dependabot rebase`) and re-evaluate — do NOT chase the symptom. Fold into the proposed `pr-blocked-why.sh` helper (tooling.md `green-pr-blocked-no-merge-signal`): annotate each red row with "run predates N develop merge(s) since — rebase may clear." Est ~15 min (habit) / ~1 h folded into the helper. Cross-ref: #1367 (stale Perf PR-fast) ↔ #1385 (the fix); `reconfirm-ci-symptom-before-acting-on-filed-diagnosis`; the `infra.md` clean-BEHIND non-advance entry.

  Details: Resuming the Android Keystore secret-at-rest PR (#1357) across a context-compaction boundary, I told the user it was "ready to merge, blocked only on stale CodeRabbit threads" — carrying the "stale" framing verbatim from the resumed summary without re-reading the threads against HEAD. Re-fetching the unresolved `reviewThreads` and diffing each against the HEAD code showed all three were current and actionable: (t2, `ConfigManager.cpp`) trusted a sibling plaintext value after a FAILED unseal of a present `*_enc` ciphertext — a real at-rest downgrade hole, fixed `0d89daca`; (t3, `SmatchetAndroidSecretBridge::Init`) left a pending JNI exception on the early `NewGlobalRef`/`GetObjectClass` failure paths — a CheckJNI-abort risk, fixed `50c8e993`; (t1) was a correct REJECT, but provably so only after reading `ApplyConfigSecretProvider`'s `if (!provider) return std::string();` fail-closed branch (`Source/Core/src/Config/ConfigManager_PathUtils.cpp` on the `feat/android-keystore-secret` head) — not because it was "stale." Merging on the inherited "stale" claim would have shipped two real security/correctness defects in a trust-boundary feature. Same recurrence-class as `reconfirm-ci-symptom-before-acting-on-filed-diagnosis` (this file, same date) — an inherited, confident-but-stale state label drives a wrong action — but in the PR-merge-readiness decision context, where the cost is shipping unreviewed defects, not a wrong fix.

- 2026-06-18 · orchestrator · [process] · P2 — a plan/backlog "not started"/"stale" status must be verified against MERGED CODE on develop, not against the presence/absence of an open branch or PR
  Details: While reconciling active plan-doc statuses I mismarked `jql-omnibox-search` Stream B "not started" because I checked for an OPEN branch/PR matching the slug and found none. The stream had in fact already SHIPPED — its code merged to develop under a different PR (#1261), so no open artifact existed to find. "Is there an open PR/branch for X" answers a different question than "is X's code on develop": work that has already landed leaves NO open artifact, so absence-of-open-PR reads identically to never-started, and the two get conflated. Corrected in #1350 only after re-verifying against merged code. Same root as the `AGENT_SELF_IMPROVEMENT.md` step-2 "cite a verified line" rule, applied to plan status rather than file behaviour.
  Concrete next action: when setting or auditing any plan/backlog item to "not started"/"shipped"/"stale", grep the MERGED tree for the feature's actual code BEFORE writing the status — `git log --oneline --all -S<symbol>`, `git show origin/develop:<file>`, or a targeted grep on develop — never infer "not started" from a missing open branch/PR. A shipped feature has no open artifact; absence-of-PR ≠ absence-of-code. ~5 min per claim; avoids a stale-status PR + a re-verify round.
  Status: applied (2026-06-20 roadmap campaign — shipped #1508)
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-20

- 2026-06-18 · orchestrator · [process] · P3 — piping a build/configure command into `grep`/`tail` masks its real exit code → a `cmake` configure FAILURE read as "exit 0"
  Details: Driving a dual-target build, I ran the build piped through a trailing `grep`/`tail` to trim output; the pipeline reported success while the underlying `cmake` configure had actually FAILED. Under a non-`pipefail` shell a pipeline's exit status is the LAST command's (the `grep`/`tail`), not the producer's, so a real build/configure failure reads as exit 0 and the agent proceeds on a broken tree. Cost a misdiagnosis round before the true configure error surfaced by reading the full unfiltered output file.
  Concrete next action: never gate a build/configure/test decision on the exit code of a command piped into `grep`/`tail`/`head`. Capture the producer's status explicitly (`cmd > out.log 2>&1; rc=$?; … ; grep … out.log`), enable `set -o pipefail`, or read the full output file. Reinforces the standing guidance to avoid trailing `grep`/`tail` over Bash output. ~0 cost once habitual.
  Status: applied (2026-06-21 P3 sweep — shipped #1524)
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-21

- 2026-06-16 · orchestrator (dead-session salvage) · [tooling] · P2 — `merge-snapshot-ledger-uncommitted-on-shared-tree-loss-risk`: `smatchet-merge-watcher` appends each merged-PR audit row to `docs/self-improvement/merge-snapshots.jsonl` in the **working copy** of the shared integration tree (C:/Dev/Smatchet) on **whatever branch happens to be checked out there**, leaving the rows uncommitted; they only reach `develop` via an irregular **manual** `chore(ledger)` harvest PR — so a `git reset`/`checkout`/`clean` of that tree (which the concurrent-session worktree rules actively encourage for a stale shared tree) silently destroys every un-harvested row.
  Details: observed taking over the dead `codex/tooltip-wheel-router` session — the shared tree's `merge-snapshots.jsonl` carried **28 un-harvested rows** (PRs 1249–1315, dated 2026-06-15) sitting dirty on a feature branch that was 66 commits behind `develop`. The watcher's PASS handler appends a JSON row per merge to whatever working copy it runs against; nothing commits or pushes it. Harvesting is a human ritual (precedent: PR #1251, #1170, #1189 — `chore(ledger): commit accumulated merge-snapshot rows`), fired on no fixed cadence. The hazard is the collision with AGENTS.md § Concurrent interactive sessions: that section tells agents to `reset --hard`/`checkout` a stale shared tree to recover HEAD drift, and `git-janitor`/`merge-watcher` auto-act on it — any of those on a tree holding un-harvested rows is **unrecoverable audit-data loss** (the rows exist nowhere else; they are not on a branch, not pushed, not stashed). In this incident the 28 rows were rescued by copying them to a temp file before any reset and replaying them into a fresh worktree off develop (this PR carries them), but that only worked because a human noticed; the normal stale-tree-recovery path would have dropped them. Two independent gaps: (a) the rows live only in an uncommitted working copy on a rug-pullable shared tree; (b) the harvest is manual + cadence-less, so the backlog of un-committed rows grows unbounded between harvests, widening the loss window.
  Concrete next action: make the ledger write durable instead of relying on a manual harvest — preferred: the merge-watcher PASS handler commits the appended row directly to a dedicated, never-reset ledger branch (or opens/updates a standing `chore(ledger)` PR) so each row is pushed the moment it is written; cheaper interim: (1) a `git-janitor`/`merge-watcher` pre-reset guard that refuses to `reset`/`checkout`/`clean` a shared tree whose `merge-snapshots.jsonl` has uncommitted rows (stash-to-temp + warn instead), and (2) a scheduled harvest (issue-janitor cadence) that auto-opens the `chore(ledger)` PR at N≥threshold un-harvested rows so the backlog can't silently accumulate. Est ~1.5-2 h for the watcher-commits-directly path + a bats fixture (append row → assert committed+pushed, not left dirty); ~45 min for the pre-reset guard alone. Cross-ref: PR #1251 / #1170 / #1189 (the manual harvest precedent this would automate); AGENTS.md § Concurrent interactive sessions (the `reset --hard`/`checkout` stale-tree recovery this collides with); `process-rules.md` § Git/p4 discipline (the 5-step destructive-op pre-flight a guard would extend).
  Status: applied (2026-06-20 roadmap campaign — shipped #1505)
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-20

- 2026-06-16 · orchestrator (PR-review sweep) · [tooling] · P3 — `appcontroller-fan-in-ratchet-count-based-evades-swap`: the new AppController include-fan-in ratchet gate is COUNT-based, not SET-based, so a count-neutral includer swap (drop one existing `#include "AppController.h"` site + add a different NEW one in the same PR) slips through silently — defeating the gate's own stated "block a new includer on sight" contract
  Details: surfaced re-reviewing PR #1308 (the PR that landed the gate). On origin/develop the script is `agents/scripts/core/appcontroller_fan_in_audit.py`; `regression()` lives at lines 181-194 (note: #1312 rewrote/robustified the selftest AFTER #1308 and bumped `BASELINE_FAN_IN` 114→115, which is why the live location no longer matches #1308's PR-diff line numbers). The body short-circuits to pass whenever `len(head_paths) <= len(base_paths)` (lines 186-187: `if len(head_paths) <= len(base_paths): return []  # ratchet down or flat — never a regression by count`). The true offender-listing loop below it already computes the set-difference `sorted(head_paths - base_paths)` (line 189) — but the count guard pre-empts it for any count-neutral change. So a PR that removes one already-counted includer and adds a different brand-new one (net count flat) returns `[]` before the set-diff loop is ever reached: the new dependency evades the gate. This contradicts the script docstring + the AGENTS.md enforcement-card intent ("a new includer is an exact signal" / "block a new includer on sight"). FAIL-OPEN — a fan-in regression-by-substitution lands unblocked. (Routing: not a user-observable product defect — an agent-system gate weakness → tooling, no GitHub Issue per ADR-0014.)
  Concrete next action: drop the count-based early return in `regression()` and ALWAYS evaluate the set-difference — `new = sorted(head_paths - base_paths)`, run each through the existing deviation-suppression check, and FAIL on any surviving new includer regardless of whether the total count rose, fell, or stayed flat. A genuine swap-and-stay-flat then either ratchets the baseline DOWN (the PR also removed one — fine) or is correctly caught as a new dependency. Add a selftest case mirroring the existing fixtures: `base={A}`, `head={B}` (equal cardinality, A dropped + B added) MUST still flag B. Est ~30-45 min (logic delta + selftest fixture + a quick `--selftest` run). Cross-ref: PR #1308 (introduced the gate); PR #1312 (`appcontroller-fan-in selftest robust… 114→115` — the rewrite that moved `regression()` to its current lines, NOT the bug source); the AGENTS.md enforcement contract-card row that documents the "new includer on sight" contract this swap defeats.
  Status: applied (2026-06-21 P3 sweep — shipped #1522)
  Last-reviewed: 2026-06-21

- 2026-06-16 · orchestrator (PR-review sweep) · [tooling] · P2 — `fuzz-target-include-closure-unresolved-invisible`: a `smatchet_add_fuzz_target` (standalone `add_executable`) whose `INCLUDES` omits a subdir that one of its closure `.cpp`s reaches via a transitive **quote-include** compiles fine in the main build (resolved by `SmatchetCoreInterface`'s INTERFACE include dirs, which the standalone target does NOT inherit) but `fatal error: file not found` on the fuzz lane — and that lane (`fuzz-smoke.yml`) is **advisory** (not required, not on `MERGE_GATES_BLOCK_ALLOWLIST_RE`), so the break can land red-but-unblocked on develop
  Details: surfaced reviewing PR #1301 (Slice E2b fuzz drivers). Two of three new targets in `tests/fuzz/CMakeLists.txt` are wired to fail compilation: `fuzz_callstack` INCLUDES only `${_fuzz_core}/include`, but its closure pulls `CallstackParser.h:4` → bare `#include "ConfigManager.h"` (header at `Source/Core/include/Config/ConfigManager.h`) → unresolved with no `include/Config` on the path; `fuzz_markdown_adf` compiles `src/Privacy/TextRedaction.cpp:1` → bare `#include "TextRedaction.h"` (header at `include/Privacy/TextRedaction.h`) with INCLUDES = `include` + `include/Ui` only → unresolved. This class is invisible to `test-lint-rules.sh` (no rule inspects CMake include-closure resolution) and only manifests on the advisory lane, so it slips the required-gate set entirely. (Routing note: the two #1301 breaks themselves are NOT logged — per the author's decision they are fixed in-PR before merge; this entry is only the systemic-gate gap.)

Est ~1-1.5 h for the static-resolver + a bats fixture (target with a closure `.cpp` whose quote-include is outside INCLUDES → WARN; fully-covered target → clean); ~10 min for the checklist-only mitigation. Cross-ref: `bucket-lane-status-broken-sentinel-auditable` (sibling — another advisory-lane break invisible to the audit/poller layer).

- 2026-06-15 · orchestrator (dx12 phase 1) · [debt] · P3 — `CMakeLists.txt` dead `if(SMATCHET_EMBEDDED_IN_UNREAL)` *variable* branch (`cmake-embedded-unreal-dead-var-branch`)
  Details: `CMakeLists.txt:311` opens `if(SMATCHET_EMBEDDED_IN_UNREAL)` (body: `set(SMATCHET_WITH_WHISPER OFF)` + `set(SMATCHET_WHISPER_LOCAL_BACKEND OFF)`) which tests a CMake **variable** that is never `set()`, `option()`'d, or passed `-D` anywhere — verified repo-wide. `SMATCHET_EMBEDDED_IN_UNREAL` exists only as (a) the target-level compile **define** `SMATCHET_EMBEDDED_IN_UNREAL=1` on `SmatchetCore_DX12` at `CMakeLists.txt:1421` (`target_compile_definitions`; the entry previously said `:1375` — line drift, corrected), and (b) `#ifndef SMATCHET_EMBEDDED_IN_UNREAL` C-preprocessor guards in product/test code. So the `:311` branch is unreachable dead config: it never fires from any configure.
  **Sharpened 2026-06-17 — this is worse than "dead + harmless", it is dead + redundant + a false-reassurance doc hazard.** The Whisper-OFF-for-Unreal invariant the `:311` body intends is in fact enforced by two OTHER live mechanisms, never by `:311`: (1) the hidden `_smatchet-light-features` preset (`CMakePresets.json` ~:38-47 sets `SMATCHET_WITH_WHISPER=OFF`), inherited by every Unreal preset (`vs-unreal-msvc` :53, `ninja-iter-unreal-msvc` :272); and (2) explicit `-DSMATCHET_WITH_WHISPER=OFF` on the CI DX12 build (`build-and-test.yml` ~:380, then `--target SmatchetCore_DX12` ~:393) and the packaging-graph smoke (~:1356). Because `:311` is dead, a *default top-level configure* leaves `SMATCHET_WITH_WHISPER` ON → `:1275` puts `SMATCHET_WITH_WHISPER=1` on the `SmatchetCoreInterface` INTERFACE → inherited by `SmatchetCore_DX12` (`smatchet_configure_core_impl_target` links it PUBLIC at `:1332`, reached via `:1446`). So an ad-hoc DX12 configure that does NOT go through a `*-unreal-*` preset and does NOT pass the explicit flag would compile the `#if defined(SMATCHET_WITH_WHISPER)` branches in CORE_SOURCES (SmatchetUI.cpp, AppController.cpp) into the Unreal-embedded lib — exactly the "header path pollution" the `:309-310` comment claims is prevented. CI never hits this (it always passes the flag), so there is **no shipped defect** — but the documented safety net does not work, and THREE comments credit the dead guard for what the preset/flag actually do: `:309-310` ("Unreal-packaged DX12 artifacts intentionally ship without Whisper … Mirror SMATCHET_WITH_MCP_UNREAL"), `:1568` ("DX12 / Unreal builds keep SMATCHET_WITH_WHISPER=OFF and never see this file"), and `build-and-test.yml:385-388` ("SMATCHET_EMBEDDED_IN_UNREAL forces Whisper off (CMakeLists.txt)"). Originally surfaced during DX12 Phase-1 work (plan `docs/plans/dx12-standalone-win-arm64.md` § Out of scope); deferred then, fully diagnosed now.
  Concrete next action (route to build-doctor — touches dual-target CMake + needs a reconfigure): (1) delete the dead `:311` `if()`/`endif()` block — now proven dead AND redundant (the light-features preset + the explicit CI `-D` both enforce Whisper-OFF for every real Unreal build path); (2) correct the three stale comments above to credit the real mechanism (`_smatchet-light-features` preset inheritance + explicit `-DSMATCHET_WITH_WHISPER=OFF` on the DX12 CI steps), not `SMATCHET_EMBEDDED_IN_UNREAL`; (3) optionally harden the invariant against an ad-hoc no-preset DX12 configure — simplest is to document that the Unreal path MUST configure through a `*-unreal-*` preset (a configure-time guard is awkward because DX12-target existence is gated by `if(WIN32)`, not a feature toggle). Verify with a `ninja-iter-unreal-msvc` reconfigure + `--target SmatchetCore_DX12` build (must stay green) and confirm no other consumer reads the phantom variable. ~30-45 min.
  Resolution: applied — the dead `if()` block is deleted; `CMakeLists.txt` keeps only the per-target compile-define (:1488) plus a corrected comment (:366-369) stating the old variable branch was dead code and Whisper is gated per-target; the last stale credit in `.github/workflows/build-and-test.yml` (~:857) is reworded in this PR to credit the explicit `-DSMATCHET_WITH_WHISPER=OFF` on the configure step.
  Status: applied
  Last-reviewed: 2026-07-08

  Details: PR #1265 (`Stabilize spawned UI test runner`) touched five code/CI/test files (`.github/workflows/build-and-test.yml`, `UiTestScenario.{h,cpp}`, `StandaloneAppBootstrap.cpp`, and a bucket-E regression test).
